My awk regex `[a-z][a-z0-9.]*\t` should match `nop\t`. The issue is likely that "nop" lines like `00000000 \tnop` — RSTART positions... the earlier uniq.py used a regex requiring `\t([0-9a-fA-F]{8})\t([a-z0-9.]+)(.*)`. For the `400128:` line: `  400128:\t00000000 \tnop` — the hex has a trailing space before tab? In cat -A: `^I00000000 ^Inop` — there's a space after the hex (because objdump aligns operands). So the regex `\t([0-9a-fA-F]{8})\t` fails because after the 8 hex digits there's a space, then tab. That's why those lines were "??". Let me fix the regex (allow spaces).
[tool-2f1] exec_command <- List distinct encodings with fixed regex ($ cat > /tmp/uniq2.py <<'EOF' import re, struct, collections info={} for line in open('/tmp/full_disasm.txt'): m=re.match(r'\s*([0-9a-fA-F]+):\t([0-9a-fA-F]{8})(\s*)\t([a-z0-9.]+)(.*)$', line) if m: addr=int(m.group(1),16); hx=m.group(2); mn=m.group(4); ops=m.group(5).strip() info[addr]=(hx,mn,ops) d=open('/app/doomgeneric/doomgeneric/doomgeneric_mips','rb').read() base=0x400110; text_off=0x110; text_size=0x3dbd0 enc=collections.defaultdict(list) for off in range(text_off, text_off+text_size, 4): w=struct.unpack_from('<I',d,off)[0] addr=base+(off-text_off) if addr in info: hx,mn,ops=info[addr] enc[(mn,hx)].append(addr) else: enc[('??',f'{w:08x}')].append(addr) print("total distinct encodings:", len(enc)) un=0 for (mn,hx),addrs in sorted(enc.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[0][1]): if mn=='??': un+=len(addrs) continue print(f"{mn:12s} {hx} x{len(addrs):5d} e.g. {addrs[0]:08x}") print("unmatched:", un) EOF python3 /tmp/uniq2.py 2>&1 | tail -160)

[tool-7f9] exec_command <- Map FPU insns to functions ($ cat > /tmp/fpus.py <<'EOF' import re # parse function labels and instruction lines funcs=[] # (addr,name) ins=[] # (addr,mn) for line in open('/tmp/full_disasm.txt'): m=re.match(r'\s*([0-9a-fA-F]+) <(.+)>:', line) if m: funcs.append((int(m.group(1),16),m.group(2))); continue m=re.match(r'\s*([0-9a-fA-F]+):\t([0-9a-fA-F]{8})(\s*)\t([a-z0-9.]+)', line) if m: a=int(m.group(1),16); mn=m.group(4) if mn in ('add.s','sub.s','mul.s','div.s','neg.s','abs.s','cvt.s.w','cvt.w.s','trunc.w.s','cvt.d.s','cvt.s.d','c.olt.s','c.olt.d','c.ult.s','c.ult.d','mov.s','mov.d','movt.s','movt.d','lwc1','swc1','ldc1','sdc1','mtc1','mfc1','mthc1','mfc0','bc1f','bc1t','c.eq.s','c.eq.d','c.lt.s','c.lt.d','c.le.s','c.le.d','c.ne.s','c.ne.d'): ins.append((a,mn)) def findfunc(a): best=None for fa,fn in funcs: if fa<=a: best=(fa,fn) else: break return best for a,mn in ins: fa,fn=findfunc(a) print(f"{a:08x} {mn:8s} in {fn} at {fa:08x}") EOF python3 /tmp/fpus.py | sort -t' ' -k5 | head -60)

- mtc1 (move to cop1): gpr→fpr
- mfc1: fpr→gpr
- mthc1 (move to high fpr)
- lwc1/ldc1/sdc1: load/store FP (word/double)
- cvt.s.w (word to single)
- cvt.d.s (single to double)
- trunc.w.s (single to word trunc)
- add.s, mul.s, div.s, neg.s, mov.s, mov.d, neg.d, c.olt.s, c.olt.d, movt.s, movt.d, bc1t

This is a manageable FPU subset. I should implement these to be safe.

Now let me look at the `teq` instruction (funct 52 in SPECIAL, "teq" per objdump). It's not standard MIPS. Let me examine its encoding: at 0x401808: 002001f4 teq at,zero,0x7. Word = 0x002001f4 = 000000 00000 01000 00000 001111 110100? Let me properly decode fields:
- op = 0
- rs = (0x002001f4 >> 21) & 0x1f = 0x1 = at? wait >>21: 0x002001f4 >> 21 = 0x1 (since 0x200000*1 = 0x200000; 0x2001f4-0x200000=0x1f4 < 0x200000). Actually 0x002001f4 = 2097652; /0x200000 = 1.0009..., so >>21 = 1. So rs = 1 (at)?? But objdump says destination 0x7 and "at,zero,0x7". Hmm let me recompute: 0x002001f4 >> 21 = 2097652 >> 21. 2097152 = 0x200000. 2097652 - 2097152 = 500 = 0x1f4. So >>21 = 1. rs = 1.
- rt = >>16 &0x1f = (2097652>>16)&31 = 32 & 31? 2097652>>16 = 32 (0x00200000>>16=0x20=32, plus 0x1f4>>16=0). rt=0 (zero).
- rd = >>11 &31 = (2097652>>11)&31 = 1024 &31 = 0x400>> ... 2097652>>11=1024; 1024&31=0. rd=0.
- sa (bits 10:6) = (word>>6)&31 = (2097652>>6)&31 = 32775 &31 = 32775 mod 32 = 7. sa=7.
- funct = word&0x3f = 0x34 = 52.

So encoding: op=0, rs=1(at), rt=0, rd=0, sa=7, funct=0x34. objdump prints "teq at,zero,0x7" — where "0x7" is sa? Teq rd,rs,rt? Hmm, objdump mnemonic ordering for "teq" appears as `teq at,zero,0x7`, so first operand=at (rs?), second=zero (rt?), third=0x7. Not obvious. What is `teq`? It's not in standard MIPS. Wait — does objdump perhaps print "teq" for the R5900 "Special2"? No.

Hmm, actually "teq" might be a GCC-specific pseudo or the "MIPS-3D"? Let me think: maybe it's from the `movn`/`movz` family? No.

Let me search the mips.patch or sources for hints: maybe the Makefile uses a different objdump that aliases. But whatever — the semantics: funct 0x34. Hmm, I remember now! MIPS has (in the "MIPS32r2" spec) the instructions `seq`/`sne` as part of... no. Actually there IS an instruction `seq`/`sne` (set equal/ not equal) in some MIPS variants (from "MIPS-32 R2"? no). Let me check: R5900 has `seq`/`sne` (funct 0x2c? no).

000000 00000 00000 rd 00000 001101  BREAK
000000 00010 00000 rd shamt 000000  SLL

000000 rs rt rd 00000 100000  NOP?
